# ENHANCEMENTS.md — dreaming.press product backlog

A living list of product/UX improvements, mined from the best media sites (NYT,
The Guardian, The Verge, Stratechery, The Pudding, FT, Bloomberg, Axios) and
adapted to a small Node/Express SSR + SQLite publication. Read this before adding
rows so we build on prior work and never duplicate. Execute the highest-value
`todo` items one by one; mark `doing` → ship → `done` with the date.

| Idea | Source / Rationale | Priority | Status | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`theme-color` + `color-scheme` meta, synced to the active theme (NYT/Verge/Guardian mobile-chrome parity)** | 2026-07-03 run (3rd of the day). **Part B (product).** With the comparison-cluster backlog drained and the two open todos blocked (MCP-spec refresh not actionable until 2026-07-28; RTL is Low), audited `head()` against standard-publisher `<head>` conventions and found a real gap: the site shipped a full light/dark theme system (`data-theme`, localStorage, `--paper` #faf7f1/#0e0d0b) but **no `theme-color` meta** (so mobile Safari/Chrome painted the address bar a default color that clashed with the paper background) and **no `color-scheme` meta** (so native form controls/scrollbars couldn't render for both schemes). Added both to `head()`, placed BEFORE `THEME_BOOT` so its `querySelector` resolves. Made the chrome *track the actual theme*, not just OS preference: since `THEME_BOOT` resolves the theme from `?theme`/localStorage/`"light"` (it deliberately ignores `prefers-color-scheme`), a static `theme-color` or a `media=` pair would desync from a dark-*saved* theme on a light-OS device — so both `THEME_BOOT` (on load) and `dpTheme()` (on toggle) now set the meta's `content` to the resolved theme's `--paper`. Default `content` matches light `--paper` for the pre-JS paint. 2 `render.test.js` regressions: (1) both metas present + theme-color precedes the boot script + boot script syncs to the dark hex; (2) a **drift guard** that reads `style.css` and asserts the two hexes still equal the `:root`/`[data-theme="dark"]` `--paper` values (so a future palette change can't silently leave the chrome stale). Full suite 1811→1813 green (+2). Env (recurring, per prior notes): fresh-clone `npm install` aborts on `canvas` gyp until `apt-get update && apt-get install -y libpango1.0-dev libcairo2-dev libjpeg-dev libgif-dev` (canvas failure also blocks better-sqlite3 → run `npm install --omit=dev` first to unblock ingest+tests, then build canvas for `gen-art.js`); `/api/analytics` unreachable so demand ran on corpus-gap + live WebSearch/WebFetch. | Medium | done | 2026-07-03 |

| **Close the cluster-orphan guard's `null` blind spot for single-model teardowns (#15/#29)** | 2026-07-02 run. **Part B (product/finding, actionable).** Part A shipped `minimax-m3-open-weight-1m-context`, a single-model teardown (not a `-vs-`/`best-`/`how-to-` slug). `clusterLabelFor` returned **null** → the piece orphaned out of the topic-cluster graph, and `check-content`'s `orphanWarnings` guard did **not** catch it: that guard only flags pieces the cluster engine buckets into the "More comparisons" CATCH-ALL (`clusterLabelFor === COMPARISON_CATCHALL`), so a demand-grade piece that returns **null** (recognized as substantive by a human but not a comparison by `isComparisonPost`) sails straight through the gate. Worked around this run per-piece by adding a real `compare:` table (promotes it to a comparison → homes it + pulls in the full summary/faq/internal-link standard). But the general fix is subtle and all the obvious global fixes are **unsafe** (verified empirically): (a) narrowing the bare `context` token in the *Prompts & Optimization* regex orphans legitimate context-mgmt pieces (`should-an-ai-agent-compact-its-own-context`, `ruler-vs-needle-in-a-haystack-context-length`, `context-editing-vs-compaction-for-long-running-agents`, `how-to-manage-context-in-a-long-running-agent`); (b) a "slug starts with a model-family token → home to Models & LLM APIs" rule poaches **7** correctly-homed pieces (claude-dreaming→Memory, deepseek-ocr→OCR, qwen3-embedding→RAG, gpt-researcher→Research, claude-code-vs-…→Coding, claude-agent-skills-vs-mcp→Protocols, claude-agent-sdk-vs-langgraph→Frameworks). Safe, narrow fix for a future run: extend `orphanWarnings` (advisory, changed-mode) to ALSO surface a **changed piece that `isDemandPiece` treats as demand-grade but `clusterLabelFor` returns null for AND whose slug carries a Models-cluster model-name token** — message "looks like a model teardown but homes nowhere; add a `compare:` table or a `-vs-`/`best-` slug." Require the model-name token so concept explainers (homed via `conceptSiblings`, not clusters) aren't falsely flagged. Rationale: the orphan guard exists to stop exactly this #15/#29 silent leak; its `=== CATCH-ALL` predicate has a blind spot for the `null` case that only bites the fast-growing single-model-teardown genre. **Shipped 2026-07-02 (later run):** implemented exactly as scoped. `orphanWarnings` now returns `{ file, reason }` and adds a second branch — `label === null && isDemandPiece(...) && MODEL_NAME_RE.test(dateStrippedSlug)` → `reason: "model-orphan"`. `MODEL_NAME_RE` mirrors the *model-name* tokens of the "Models & LLM APIs" cluster regex only (`gpt\|claude\|gemini\|qwen\|qwen3\|kimi\|glm\|minimax\|deepseek\|gemma`), bounded to family names. The `label === null` precondition makes it inherently safe against the poaching that killed the global fixes (a): anything that homed elsewhere via a model token — e.g. `qwen3-embedding-…` → RAG — returns non-null and is excluded before the branch runs; the model-name requirement excludes null-homed concept explainers (fix (b)). `main()` prints a distinct "looks like a single-model teardown but homes nowhere; add a `compare:` table or a `-vs-`/`best-` slug" line. Advisory only (console in `--changed`, not a failing gate), so it guides authors without blocking. 1 test added (positive minimax-m3 teardown + 2 negatives: no-model-token essay, non-demand model mention); suite 1789→1790 green. | Medium | done | 2026-07-02 |
| **Validate extension-less `/posts/<slug>` cross-links against the corpus (close a dead-link blind spot)** | 2026-07-02 run. **Part B (product/quality).** `check-content.js`'s dead-internal-link guard (added after FIXES 2026-06-23, when 30 dead cross-links shipped on the money pages the #15/#29 internal-link engine depends on) only matched the `.html`/`.md` forms — `/\]\(\/posts\/([a-z0-9-]+)\.(?:html\|md)…/`. But the house style also links the **extension-less** `/posts/<slug>` form (server.js 301-redirects the bare form to canonical `.html` when it resolves and hard-404s when it does not), so a typo'd or hallucinated extension-less sibling link would ship a live 404 completely unseen by the gate. Made the extension optional in the regex (`(?:\.(?:html\|md))?`) so the bare form is validated identically; added two regression tests (a dead bare link is flagged; a resolving bare link with `#anchor` passes). Re-ran across the corpus: `✓ all 560 posts meet the standard` (no pre-existing extension-less dead links surfaced, no false positives), full suite 1782 green (+2). Rationale: Wirecutter/Verge/NYT treat internal-link integrity as a crawl-depth + dwell lever; a validator that only covers two of the three link forms the house actually writes is a silent leak in exactly the guard meant to stop it. | Medium | done | 2026-07-02 |

| **Visible per-article revision note (`update_note`) beside the "Updated" stamp — NYT/Guardian "this story has been updated" transparency** | 2026-07-02 run. **Part B (product).** The site already lit an "Updated &lt;date&gt;" freshness stamp (from explicit `updated:` or the git last-commit date) + accurate `dateModified` JSON-LD, but it only ever said *when* a piece changed, never *what* changed — the transparency half of the NYT/Guardian/Verge correction convention, and an E-E-A-T/trust signal Google rewards. Added an optional `update_note:` frontmatter field carried end-to-end: parsed in `scripts/ingest.js`, persisted as a new `update_note TEXT` column in `lib/db.js` (added to `CREATE TABLE`, the idempotent ALTER-migration loop so existing DBs self-upgrade, and the `INSERT OR REPLACE` column/value/binding lists; `null` when absent), and rendered in `lib/render.js` as an italic `.upd-note` span appended to the `.article-updated` line — **only when `updated` differs from `date`** (so a same-day publish never shows a note) and **HTML-escaped** via `esc()` so a note can't inject markup. `.article-updated` gained `flex-wrap: wrap` so a long note reflows on mobile instead of overflowing. This directly equips the standing 2026-07-28 MCP-spec refresh todo (and any future correction) to show a reader-facing "what changed" line, not just a silent date bump. 2 tests added (db round-trip incl. null-when-absent; render shows/escapes the note and suppresses it when `updated == date`); full suite 1768→1770 green. | High | done | 2026-07-02 |
| **Make the origin CDN-ready: real `Cache-Control` on HTML + long-lived cover caching (council move #20)** | 2026-07-02 run (run 157). **Part B (product).** Audit found SSR HTML responses set **no `Cache-Control` header at all** — so no edge (Cloudflare, Fastly, a browser's heuristic cache) could safely cache a single page; the whole "CDN + caching" move was blocked not on DNS but on the origin never emitting a cache policy. Fixed in `server.js`: (1) the shared `html()` helper now stamps `public, max-age=0, s-maxage=300, stale-while-revalidate=86400` on 200 responses — browsers revalidate each navigation (a cheap 304 via Express's weak ETag), but a shared edge may serve from cache for 5 min and keep serving stale for a day while it refreshes, which is the actual TTFB win. (2) The **article route is deliberately exempted** with `private, max-age=0, must-revalidate` (set *before* `html()`, which no-ops when a policy already exists) because it calls `DB.bumpView` per real-browser request — a shared cache serving it would make real views vanish from the counter. Best-of-media pattern (NYT/Guardian/Verge all front SSR with short-TTL + SWR at the edge while keeping personalized/counted routes private). (3) Slug-addressed **covers** — the LCP element on every article, generated deterministically so a given URL is effectively immutable — went from `max-age=3600` to `public, max-age=86400, stale-while-revalidate=604800` on both the AVIF/WebP negotiation branch and the `/images` static mount, taking a revalidation RTT off the LCP path without the year-staleness risk of `immutable` (an edited cover still refreshes within a day). 3 header regression tests added to `api.test.js` (hub page → `s-maxage`; article → `private` and not `public`; cover → `max-age=86400` + week SWR); verified live with `curl -D-` on `/`, `/wire.html`, `/tools`, the article, the cover PNG, and the AVIF variant. Full suite 1763→1766 green. #20 stays 🔵 only for the owner's Cloudflare DNS toggle — the origin now emits headers an edge will honor. | High | done | 2026-07-02 |

Sharp split: continuation is prose-safe but structure-hostile (truncated JSON is unparseable; "continue" re-generates lossily → Gemini batch invalid JSON), so structured calls discard-and-retry with headroom. | **#28 fourth calculator — `/calculators/context-budget` (context-window budget estimator)** | The `/calculators/` namespace answered three of the four questions you ask before shipping an agent — will it fit (VRAM), what will it cost (token bill), how fast will it feel (latency) — but not the fourth and most agent-specific: **how much context does the agent actually get, and when will it run out.** This is a high-intent query family ("LLM context window budget", "when does my agent need to compact", "how many turns fit in 200k tokens"). Shipped `/calculators/context-budget` to complete the capacity→price→speed→**context** set. Pure unit-tested math in `lib/calc.js` (`contextBudgetEstimate` + `CONTEXT_PRESETS`), server-rendered default mirrored by an inline client and locked by a no-drift render test — identical to the VRAM/cost/latency pattern. The modelled insight (one non-obvious idea per artifact, same bar as the articles): an agent **never gets the whole window** — the system prompt + tool/function schemas + always-on memory are re-sent every turn and sit in front of the first message, and you must hold back an output reserve that is *also* an accuracy reserve (Chroma's context-rot finding: recall degrades as the window fills). What's left divides by tokens-per-turn into a finite **turn count before compaction** — the verdict surfaces "N agent steps before you must compact" + "% lost to fixed overhead" (72 turns / 6% at 200K; collapses on a 32K model or a fat MCP tool catalog, which is the real-world failure mode). Math clamps usable at 0 when overhead exceeds the window and guards both divisors. Wired: route, footer link, sitemap entry (`TOOL_URLS` 5→6), `WebApplication` schema, cross-links to the compaction / context-rot / context-engineering / too-many-tools articles + all three sibling calculators, Anthropic "Effective context engineering" + Chroma "Context Rot" sources. Closes the data-engine calculator backlog. Suite **1567→1572 green**. | High | done | 2026-06-30 |
| **#28 third calculator — `/calculators/llm-latency` (latency / throughput estimator)** | The `/calculators/` namespace already answered two of the three questions you ask before serving a model — will it fit (VRAM) and what will it cost (token bill) — but not the third, **how fast will it feel**, which is a high-intent query family ("LLM time to first token", "tokens per second to latency", "why is my agent slow"). Shipped `/calculators/llm-latency` to complete the capacity→price→speed trilogy. Pure unit-tested math in `lib/calc.js` (`llmLatencyEstimate` + `LATENCY_PRESETS`), server-rendered default mirrored by an inline client and locked by a no-drift render test — identical to the VRAM/cost pattern. The modelled insight (one non-obvious idea per artifact, same bar as the articles): a chat reply pays TTFT once and is decode-dominated, but an **agent serializes many short turns over a growing context**, so end-to-end latency = (overhead + prompt/prefill_rate + output/decode_rate) × sequential_turns — the TTFT tax is paid once per turn and dominates the wall-clock, which the verdict surfaces as "% of time spent waiting, not generating" (62% at the defaults). A high-tokens/sec model can lose to a snappier-TTFT one in a loop. Found + fixed a genuine divide-by-zero in the shared `num()` clamp (negative rate → `min=0` before the allowZero guard → Infinity) via `min:1` on the two rate divisors. Wired: route, footer link, sitemap entry (`TOOL_URLS` 4→5), `WebApplication` schema, cross-links to TTFT-vs-TPOT / prefill-vs-decode / reduce-agent-latency + both sibling calculators, Databricks + NVIDIA sources. Speeds are editable order-of-magnitude defaults, not a benchmark. Suite **1550→1567 green**; `check:content` clean. | High | done | 2026-06-30 |

| **`isDemandPiece` blind spot: topic-led Wire pieces escape the SEO-completeness standard (#7/#14 side effect)** | `scripts/check-content.js` classifies a Wire/Stack piece as a "demand piece" (held to summary+faq+compare+art+internal-link) only when its slug carries `-vs-` OR it declares a `compare:` block. But council #14 mandates *topic-led* headlines (NOT an "X vs Y" slug), so a genuinely high-intent demand piece written to that rule (e.g. `opentelemetry-genai-semantic-conventions`, shipped this run) is invisible to the gate and can ship with no `summary`/`faq`/`compare` — the exact rich-result kit the pivot depends on. Caught this run only because the author manually upgraded the piece. **Caution before acting:** the existing `-vs-`/`compare:`/`best-`/`how-to-` signals are deliberately unambiguous to avoid catching the desk's metaphorical Wire essays ("the-megawatt-you-cannot-rent"); any broadened heuristic must NOT flag those, and `--changed` mode already grandfathers committed pieces so a fix only affects new pieces. Possible direction: treat a Wire/Stack piece with a `sources:` line of ≥N real URLs AND a comparison-shaped title as demand — but validate against the full corpus for false positives before shipping. **SHIPPED (2026-06-29, run 128):** chose the principled, provably-safe fix over the risky title heuristic — **aligned `isDemandPiece` with the cluster engine's own `isComparisonPost` (`lib/db.js`)**, which already homes `best-…`/`how-to-…` slugs (not just `…-vs-…`) in topic clusters and gives them sibling rails, yet they escaped the SEO gate — a genuine inconsistency between two surfaces that should agree (a piece that earns a cluster home must ship the kit). `isDemandPiece` now returns true for a Wire/Stack piece with a `compare:` table **OR** a query-shaped slug (`(^|-)vs(-|$)` / `best-` / `how-to-`, the exact vocab db.js admits and corpus-validated there as essay-safe) **OR** a `faq:` block. The `faq:` signal is the load-bearing addition for council #14 (topic-led headlines, non-query slugs): a People-Also-Ask block is the search-intent tell an essay never writes. **Corpus-validated before shipping** (366 Wire/Stack posts, analysis script): of the 48 pieces that escaped the old gate, the 13 newly classified as demand are ALL genuine demand pieces (llm-as-a-judge, semantic-caching-for-ai-agents, mcp-authorization-oauth, best-chunking/embedding/reranker/vector-db, context-engineering, gartner-spending, how-to-{authenticate,build,deploy,test}-mcp-server) and **ZERO** are the desk's metaphorical essays — those carry only a `summary:` takeaway (used corpus-wide, so deliberately NOT a demand signal), never a `faq:`, and their slugs are non-query (`the-megawatt-you-cannot-rent`). The honest boundary (documented in code): a pure-prose topic-led piece with none of these signals is structurally indistinguishable from an essay, so it stays author-discipline — broadening further would flag the council-voice essays. `--changed` grandfathers the 13 committed pieces (build stays green; they're now visible to `--strict`/future re-touch). Pinned with a `content-standard.test.js` regression (faq-bearing topic-led + best-/how-to- slugs → demand with full-kit errors firing; summary-only metaphorical essay → NOT demand). | **Google News sitemap (`/news-sitemap.xml`, `<news:news>`) — surface fresh Wire pieces to Top Stories / Google News (#1 discovery, #17 cadence)** | A publication shipping a dated, non-fiction piece every run wants its newest articles in Top Stories, but the site declared only the standard `sitemap.xml` (with the image extension) — no Google News sitemap, the canonical discovery aid for *recently published* URLs. Implemented `newsSitemapXml(posts, now?)` in `lib/pages.js` (pure → unit-tested): lists each recent article's `<loc>` + `<news:publication><news:name>dreaming.press</news:name><news:language>en</news:language></news:publication>` + `<news:publication_date>` (W3C date) + escaped `<news:title>`, newest-first, capped at the 1000-URL news-sitemap limit. **Window anchor (load-bearing):** the build's `NOW` constant is hand-rolled and lags reality (`2026-06-13`) and the deploy clock isn't reachable from a pure fn, so the 2-day window keys off the **freshest published date in the corpus**, not wall-clock — with the daily cadence that tracks "today", and if the site goes dark, stale entries fall out of Google's own 48h cutoff anyway (harmless). **Satire excluded:** `section === "fabrications"` is filtered out — labeled satire must never be submitted as news; every other section is dated non-fiction and eligible. Wired the `/news-sitemap.xml` route in `server.js` and added a second `Sitemap:` line to root `robots.txt` so crawlers discover it. Note: during the late-June bulk-import backfill the window currently holds ~91 entries (many posts share 06-26/-27/-28 dates); it self-corrects to a handful as the daily cadence ages older dates past 48h. Locked with 3 `pages.test.js` units (well-formedness + namespace; 48h-window/satire-exclusion/escaping/newest-first via synthetic dates; explicit-`now` anchor + empty-but-valid urlset) and a `/news-sitemap.xml` route test. | **Expose the verifiable `sources:` list as schema.org `citation` nodes on every article (E-E-A-T / GEO sourcing signal)** | The publication's first house rule is that Wire/Stack pieces must cite **real, verifiable sources**, and they already render as a visible numbered reference list + inline citation markers — but the structured-data graph said **nothing** about them. The NewsArticle/TechArticle JSON-LD carried rich signals (`about`+`sameAs`, author `@id` E-E-A-T reconciliation, `speakable`, `wordCount`, `timeRequired`) yet had **no `citation`**, so a crawler or an AI answer engine had no machine-readable signal that a piece is sourced or which authoritative works it rests on. Scholarly publishers, NYT/Guardian fact pieces, and Wikipedia all expose references as structured data; `schema.org/citation` is a recognized `Article`/`CreativeWork` property, and demonstrable sourcing is exactly the trust signal Google's quality systems and LLM answer surfaces (the GEO audience this site explicitly writes "for AI agents") weigh. Fix (`lib/render.js`, one guarded spread mirroring the existing `about`/`keywords` pattern): emit `citation: p.sources.map(([url,label]) => ({"@type":"CreativeWork", name:label, url}))` — built from the SAME parsed `[url,label]` source pairs the visible reference list and inline `citeLinks` use, so the structured data can never disagree with what's on the page. Additive + guarded: absent/empty sources ⇒ no `citation` key, so Dispatches/Fabrications (which carry none) emit nothing rather than an empty array. Verified live on this run's Wire piece (7 `CreativeWork` citation nodes, each with the real source URL). Locked with a new `render.test.js` regression: a sourced piece emits exactly one CreativeWork per source with a real http(s) URL drawn from the post's declared sources, and a source-less piece emits no `citation` property. Suite **1403 green**. | Medium | done | 2026-06-28 |

| **Stop descriptive compare-table column labels from leaking into an article's schema.org `about` entity graph** | The `about` array on each Article (#25) is meant to name the real *things* a piece compares (LangGraph, Qdrant, …) so the knowledge graph resolves the page to specific entities. But concept/how-to pages reuse the same at-a-glance grid for a *descriptive* axis ("Model \| You charge for \| Breaks when"), and the prior negative filter only rejected a header cell when it **led** with an article/interrogative/auxiliary. That missed two big classes of prose label: ones led by a **pronoun** ("You charge for") and ones that **trail off on a bare connective** ("Best for", "Reach for it when", "Scales to", "Optimize for", "Protects against") — so ~26 pages were publishing bogus `Thing`s like *"Best for"*, *"Breaks when"*, *"Available on"* into their entity graph, exactly the pollution the filter exists to prevent. Fix (`lib/render.js`): factor the rule into an exported `isDescriptiveLabel(name)` that flags a cell as a label when (after stripping any parenthetical alias list) it **leads** with an article/interrogative/auxiliary/pronoun OR **ends** with a dangling connective. Both signals are token-anchored (`^word\b` / `\bword$`), so glued names survive untouched — "Notion" keeps its "on", "Speech-to-speech"/"End-to-end" their "to" — and domain-shaped names are protected by excluding "so"/"as"/"at"/"by" from the trailing set, so "MCP.so" stays an entity. High precision by construction: a corpus sweep confirmed it newly drops **32 genuine prose labels across ~26 pages and zero real entities** (incl. the compared *concepts* like "Tensor parallel"/"Canary"/"Blue-green", which correctly remain). The render test now imports the same predicate (mirror can't drift) and a new unit test pins 17 label cases and 12 entity cases (domains, version tags, parenthetical alias lists). Suite **1364 green**. | High | done | 2026-06-27 |
| **Opt every HTML page into large image previews + full snippets via `<meta name="robots" content="max-image-preview:large, max-snippet:-1, max-video-preview:-1">`** | Google's default `max-image-preview` is `standard` (a small thumbnail), and `max-image-preview:large` is effectively a prerequisite for **Google Discover** eligibility and for the large cover thumbnail in Image/Search results — every major publisher (NYT/Guardian/Verge/Axios) sets it. This is the natural completion of the cover-investment thread: the repo now generates a distinctive PNG+WebP+AVIF cover per post (done), declares each one in the image sitemap (done), and surfaces it in `og:image` — but it never told Google it was *allowed* to show that cover large, so the single highest-traffic discovery surface (Discover) was left on the table. Fix (`lib/render.js`): one line in the shared `<head>` template (used by every HTML page; the `.md` twins get `noindex` via headers, not this template, so nothing indexable is suppressed) emitting `index, follow, max-image-preview:large, max-snippet:-1, max-video-preview:-1` right after the meta description. Sitewide, zero per-page branching, no schema risk. Locked by a render regression test asserting the directive carries `max-image-preview:large` and stays `index, follow`. Suite **1361 green**. | High | done | 2026-06-27 |
| **Declare each article's generative cover in `sitemap.xml` via the Google image-sitemap extension (covers were crawlable but undeclared)** | Major publishers (NYT/Guardian) declare article images in their sitemaps so the artwork is eligible for Google Images and Discover — image search is a real organic-discovery surface, and this repo generates a distinctive content-driven cover (PNG+WebP+AVIF) for *every* post, yet `sitemapXml` emitted only `<loc>`/`<lastmod>` and never surfaced a single one. Exactly the same "indexable but undeclared" gap the per-author archive row closed, applied to the covers. Fix (`lib/pages.js`): give each post sitemap entry an `image` (its canonical `coverUrl()` → `/images/<slug>.png`) plus the title as `imageTitle`, declare `xmlns:image` on the `<urlset>` only when an entry uses it, and emit `<image:image><image:loc>…</image:loc><image:title>…</image:title></image:image>` per article (title XML-escaped via `esc`). Only post entries get an image, so hubs/section pages (no single canonical cover) stay plain — and `<image:loc>` doesn't match the existing `/<loc>/` count test, so per-URL `lastmod` coverage is unaffected. Locked by a new regression test asserting the namespace is declared, every post URL carries exactly one `<image:image>` with the canonical cover loc + escaped title, and the `<image:image>` count equals the post count (none leak onto hubs). Suite **1356 green**. | High | done | 2026-06-27 |

Locked by extending the sitemap count test (adds `authorPages` = distinct canonical keys) and the hub-freshness test (asserts every `/authors/:id` is present and dated to its author's freshest piece). | **De-inflate sitemap `lastmod` for the data-backed Stack pages (date them from the tool catalog, not the post `latest`)** | Google/Bing discount a sitemap whose URLs all carry the same recently-bumped date (the freshness-inflation NYT/large-site SEO guidance warns about), and the codebase already fixed this for section + cluster hubs (each tracks its OWN freshest piece). But the Stack/tool ecosystem — `/tools`, `/reports/state-of-ai-agents`, `/stack/:slug`, `/best/:category`, `/alternatives/:slug`, `/compare/:a-vs-:b` (≈70 URLs) — was still stamped with the global post `latest`, so every one of them claimed to change each time *any blog post* shipped, even though their content is the TOOLS catalog (re-synced by `sync-tools.js`), not posts. Fix (`lib/pages.js`): extracted a pure, exported `toolSitemapEntries(toolRows, fallback)` that dates each tool page from the live catalog — a tool page uses its own `synced_at` (else the repo's `pushed_at`); `/best/:category` and `/compare/:a-vs-:b` take the freshest of the tools they actually show; `/tools` + `/reports` take the catalog-wide freshest — and falls back to the post `latest` only when the catalog has no dates yet (e.g. before the first sync), so every URL keeps a plausible `lastmod`. Net effect in production: tool URLs stop moving in lock-step with the article feed and move only when the tool data does, so a re-crawl signal means something. Locked with a focused unit test feeding a synthetic catalog (one dated tool, rest undated) that asserts per-page data-driven dating, the no-inflation property (no tool URL inherits the newer post `latest` once any catalog date exists), and the before-first-sync fallback; the existing well-formed / per-article-lastmod / hub-freshness sitemap tests stay green. Suite **1347 green**. | High | done | 2026-06-27 |

The bug: the `revisit:` advisory only printed in the **full** `node scripts/check-content.js` audit — which the autonomous routine never runs. The routine runs `check:freshness` + `check:content --changed`; `--changed` only inspects the current run's slate, and `check:freshness` *deliberately* excludes timely news (its header even names `revisit:` as "the companion … which only fires for TIMELY news pieces"). So a piece that opted into `revisit:` and then came due was invisible to the loop meant to re-check it — the advisory was orphaned. Fix: added a pure `revisitDueReport(posts, today)` to `check-freshness.js` that re-uses check-content's exported `revisitDue`, and printed it as a second advisory block in the CLI (soonest-due first, non-gating) so the **one** freshness command the routine runs each cycle now surfaces BOTH age-decay and revisit-due. | `sources:` silent-degradation guard in `check-content` (mirror the faq/figures/compare guards) | **The required evidence line had no parse guard.** `check-content` already guards the other `;;`/`|`-delimited frontmatter fields against silent ingest degradation — `compareColumnMismatch`, `faqMalformed`, `figuresMalformed` — but `sources:`, the one field AGENTS.md makes **required** for every Wire/Stack piece, had none. ingest.js parses it leniently (`split(";;")` → first `|` = url → `if (url && url.trim())` keeps it), so two real typos fail silently rather than erroring: (1) a `;;` row break mistyped as a lone `|` (or a leading `|`) leaves an empty url and the whole source is **dropped** from the numbered references list — and any inline citation `citeLinks` would have matched to that URL silently breaks; (2) a `;;` mistyped as a single `;` **fuses** two sources into one chunk, so the second URL leaks into the first's label half and is lost. Both are the exact silent-regression class the existing guards exist for, on the highest-stakes field (it's the publication's evidence/trust layer + the `#26` provenance promise). Shipped `sourcesMalformed(raw)` (faithful to ingest's split: empty-url → flag; `://` in the label half → flag the fused pair; a no-label url and a trailing empty `;;` are SUPPORTED forms and pass), wired into `auditPiece` so the `--changed` gate (in `npm test`) now holds new pieces to it. Corpus-scanned: **0 of 343** existing posts flagged (no false positives). 3 regression tests (empty-url drop, single-`;` fusion, well-formed incl. no-label url + trailing `;;`). | Bounded `<meta name="description">` / `og:description` at the render layer (`metaDescription()`) | **The on-page `dek` was piped verbatim into the description tag, so 52 live pieces shipped a description over the AGENTS.md 200-char cap — which Google just truncates at ~155-160 chars, often mid-word, wasting the SERP/social snippet.** (NYT/Guardian/Axios all serve a separate, length-bounded meta description distinct from the on-page standfirst.) Pure/deterministic. Applied to BOTH `<meta name="description">` and `og:description` in `head()`, so it heals all 52 over-long pages *and* every future page at once with **zero content edits** and no change to the on-page dek. | Column-consistency gate for `compare:` tables in `check:content` + fix a live broken table (run 65, content robustness / #30) | **A silently misaligned at-a-glance table on a money page — the single most snippet-winning element for versus queries — that nothing caught.** `check-content` only ever counted `compare:` ROWS (`compareRowCount` ≥ 2); it never verified each row has the same number of CELLS as the header. So a row whose `;;` separator was typed as a lone ` \| ` fuses two rows into one over-wide row, and `render.js` (which builds the header from row 0 and a `<tr>` per data row) emits a table with more `<td>`s than `<th>`s — columns shift, the comparison reads wrong, no error. A corpus scan found exactly this **live** in `ap2-vs-x402-vs-acp-agent-payment-protocols.md`: a 4-column header with a final 8-cell row (`Needs crypto?…No \| Best for \|…` — the `;;` before "Best for" had become a bare pipe). Fixed that row (restored the `;;`), and added `compareColumnMismatch()` to `check-content.js` — it splits every row with the SAME exported `splitCells()` ingest/render use (so an escaped `\|` inside a cell, e.g. an L1 formula "Sum of \|a-b\|", is correctly NOT miscounted as a column break — the run-64 case), and flags the first row whose width ≠ the header's. Wired into `auditPiece` for any piece carrying a compare table, so it rides the existing `--strict` (full corpus) and `--changed` (this run's slate) gates. Corpus now **0 mismatches** (was 1), so the full audit enforces it cleanly. 2 regression tests (the 3-vs-6 fused-row break flagged; a consistent table with escaped pipes passes). | `sameAs` entity reconciliation on comparison `about` entities (run 48) | **Entity SEO — disambiguation beyond naming (#25 schema).** Run 47 added `about: [{@type:Thing,name}]` from the compare-table header, but a bare name still leaves a search engine / AI agent to *guess* which "Qdrant", "vLLM", or "Letta" the page means. This is the reconciliation Google's knowledge graph and answer engines reward on "X vs Y" queries: the page no longer just *names* the entities it compares, it *points at* them. Identity (name/repo) comes from the static seed, not the live star count, so it's stable. Verified live (mem0-vs-zep-vs-letta → Mem0 & Letta carry repo `sameAs`, "Zep / Graphiti" degrades to bare). 1 regression test reconstructs the catalog aliases, asserts every matched column carries the right repo `sameAs`, every untracked column stays bare, every emitted `sameAs` is a canonical GitHub repo URL, and that ≥1 real corpus piece exercises a match. | NEW demand pieces shipped (run 37) — **Late Chunking vs Contextual Retrieval** (`late-chunking-vs-contextual-retrieval`, Wire) + **MCP Sampling vs Elicitation** (`mcp-sampling-vs-elicitation`, Wire) + **HowTo JSON-LD for how-to guides (Part B feature)** | Two high-intent dev queries the 247-post corpus had never owned, both Wire (zero Dispatches; #7 cap honored; #14 topic-led headlines), researched via parallel sub-agents against primary sources and shipped at full standard (summary/faq/sources/art/in-cluster link/compare table). (1) **Late Chunking vs Contextual Retrieval** — targets "late chunking vs contextual retrieval" / "fix rag chunk context". Non-obvious thesis: the two 2024 techniques fix the SAME failure (a chunk embedded in isolation loses the document that disambiguates it) in **opposite places** — late chunking (Jina) fixes it in *vector space* (embed the whole doc first, then pool token embeddings per chunk; zero extra LLM calls, but capped by the embedding model's ~8k context window and only the dense vector benefits), Contextual Retrieval (Anthropic) fixes it in the *text* (LLM-written 50–100 token blurb prepended per chunk, ~$1.02/M doc tokens with prompt caching). Buried lede: the real axis isn't cost — late chunking only lifts the embedding, while Contextual Retrieval adds *real text* so BM25 + rerankers benefit too, which is why Anthropic's biggest win STACKS all three (failed retrievals −35% embeddings → −49% +BM25 → −67% +rerank, 5.7%→1.9%); they compose, not compete. Verified: Jina blog + arXiv:2409.04701, Anthropic /news/contextual-retrieval (all five numbers + $1.02/M verbatim), Jina Part II, Weaviate. (2) **MCP Sampling vs Elicitation** — targets "mcp sampling vs elicitation" / "what is mcp elicitation". Non-obvious thesis: MCP looks one-way (client calls server) but has a **return lane with two mirror features** — both let the server reach back through the client, but **sampling reaches the MODEL** (`sampling/createMessage`: borrow the client's LLM so the server needs no key/bill; present since 2024-11-05) and **elicitation reaches the HUMAN** (`elicitation/create`: structured user input via a restricted flat/primitive JSON Schema, accept/decline/cancel, MUST NOT request secrets; added 2025-06-18). The catch (the timely, actionable payload): both are *client* capabilities many clients still don't implement → a server that depends on either degrades silently, so design them as progressive enhancement; the 2025-11-25 revision already extended sampling (tool-calling) + elicitation (URL mode), ahead of client support. Adversarial sub-agent verification corrected the common web error that BOTH shipped in 2025-06-18 (sampling is from the original 2024-11-05 spec). Verified: MCP spec /2025-06-18/client/{sampling,elicitation} + changelog, client feature matrix, 2025-11-25 changelog. Both rail correctly into existing clusters (late-chunking→**RAG & Retrieval** via `chunking`; mcp-sampling→**Protocols (MCP & A2A)** via `mcp`) with live sibling rails — no taxonomy change needed. **Part B (#25/#26 structured data):** added **HowTo JSON-LD** for the three `how-to-` guides (`how-to-build-an-mcp-server`, `how-to-authenticate-a-remote-mcp-server`, `how-to-prevent-prompt-injection-in-ai-agents`). Steps are the piece's own `##` sections (each already a tocify deep-link anchor), with `HowToStep.text` from each section's leading prose — so the structured steps match what the reader navigates. Same precedent as the existing FAQPage emission: Google deprecated the HowTo *rich result* (2023) but the markup stays valid + consumed by Bing/AI agents, and a how-to guide IS structurally a HowTo; gated to slugs starting `how-to-` with ≥2 sections so a metaphorical "how-to" essay can't mislabel itself. Regression test pins it (HowTo emitted with ≥2 anchored steps that resolve; non-guides emit none). (1) **Neo4j vs FalkorDB vs Memgraph** — targets "graph database for graphrag" / "neo4j vs falkordb vs memgraph". Non-obvious thesis: the decision rides two axes absent from every vendor benchmark — **memory hierarchy** (Neo4j disk-backed/scales-past-RAM, Memgraph in-memory-first, FalkorDB GraphBLAS sparse matrices in a Redis process) and the **license**, which constrains shipping more than latency: Neo4j Community GPLv3 (copyleft), Memgraph Community BSL 1.1, FalkorDB SSPLv1 — all restrictive, none Apache/MIT. Buried lede: the one permissively-licensed (MIT) embedded option, **Kuzu, was archived by its sponsor Oct 2025**, so there's no actively-maintained OSI-permissive GraphRAG engine right now; FalkorDB's real edge is multi-tenancy (thousands of per-agent graphs/instance). Verified live: neo4j ~17k/GPLv3, FalkorDB ~4.6k/SSPLv1, memgraph ~4.2k/BSL, kuzu ~4k/MIT-archived; neo4j-graphrag-python + FalkorDB/GraphRAG-SDK exist (The Register + HN on the Kuzu archiving). (2) **Streamlit vs Gradio vs Chainlit** — targets "streamlit vs gradio vs chainlit" / "python ui for llm app". Thesis: they look interchangeable but each is built on a different **execution model**, which decides which job is trivial — Streamlit reruns the whole script top-to-bottom per interaction (dashboards easy, streaming chat/agent state a fight via session_state/fragments); Gradio is a functional input→fn→output event model for model demos, HF Spaces-native, now auto-exposing a REST API + MCP server; Chainlit is chat-first with the real differentiator (native agent **step/tool-call** rendering) but community-maintained since the founding team stepped back May 1 2025. Non-obvious framing: all three are Apache-2.0, so unlike the graph-DB tier license isn't the axis — fit is. | NEW demand pieces shipped (run 35) — **OpenAI Responses vs Assistants vs Chat Completions** (`openai-responses-api-vs-assistants-api-vs-chat-completions`, Wire) + **Matryoshka embeddings** (`matryoshka-embeddings`, Wire) + new **Models & LLM APIs** topic cluster | Two high-intent dev queries the 243-post corpus had never owned, both Wire (zero Dispatches; #7 cap honored; #14 topic-led headlines), researched via parallel sub-agents against primary sources and shipped at full standard (summary/faq/sources/art/in-cluster link/compare table). (1) **Responses vs Assistants vs Chat Completions** — targets "responses api vs assistants api" / "which OpenAI API for agents". Non-obvious thesis: it isn't a three-way choice — the **Assistants API is deprecated with a hard sunset Aug 26 2026** (notice Aug 26 2025, gated on Responses reaching feature parity), so the live decision is Chat Completions (stateless, portable, the industry-standard shape, supported indefinitely) vs the **Responses API** (launched Mar 2025, OpenAI's default: server-side state via `store`/`previous_response_id`, hosted built-in tools, MCP). Sharpest differentiator: Responses **preserves reasoning items across turns** (chained, or encrypted reasoning content for ZDR) which Chat Completions structurally discards — so reasoning models keep their chain of thought on Responses and rebuild it from scratch on Chat Completions. Verified: OpenAI deprecations page, Responses launch post, conversation-state guide, Cookbook reasoning-items, DevDay 2023. (Corrected the common "first half of 2026" sunset misconception to the published Aug 26 2026 date.) (2) **Matryoshka embeddings** — targets "matryoshka embeddings" / "truncate / shorten embedding dimensions". MRL (Kusupati et al., NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2205.13147) front-loads information so a vector *prefix* is itself usable — OpenAI's `dimensions` param shrinks a 3-large vector to 256 dims and still beats full-1536 ada-002 (~12× storage cut). Non-obvious payoff: **adaptive retrieval** — shortlist on a cheap truncated index, rerank only that shortlist on full vectors — recovers Supabase's 89.2%→99% accuracy at ~13% QPS cost, refusing the storage-vs-accuracy tradeoff. Two failure modes pinned: must be an MRL-trained model; renormalize after truncating. Verified: MRL paper, OpenAI embeddings post, Supabase Adaptive Retrieval benchmark, HF Matryoshka blog, Nomic Embed. **Part B (#15/#29 internal-link SEO):** the OpenAI piece exposed the biggest taxonomy gap — the `/comparisons` **catch-all held 19 pieces** with a coherent model/API-decision cluster hiding inside. Added a **"Models & LLM APIs"** cluster (placed LAST so first-match-wins protects every specific cluster) collecting `claude-vs-gpt-vs-gemini`, `qwen-vs-llama-vs-deepseek-vs-mistral-vs-gemma`, `small-language-models-vs-llms`, `mixture-of-experts-vs-dense`, `where-the-leverage-…-open-vs-closed`, and the new OpenAI piece — **catch-all 19→13**. | NEW demand pieces shipped (run 34) — **CAG vs RAG** (`cag-vs-rag`, Wire) + **GRPO vs PPO** (`grpo-vs-ppo`, Wire) + **revived the dead table-of-contents nav corpus-wide (Part B bug fix)** | Two high-demand dev queries the 241-post corpus had never owned, both Wire (zero Dispatches; #7 cap honored), shipped at full standard (summary/faq/sources/art/in-cluster link/compare table). (1) **CAG vs RAG** — targets "cache-augmented generation vs rag" / "CAG vs RAG". Non-obvious, durable thesis: the decision is NOT speed or accuracy but **corpus size × mutability** — CAG preloads the whole knowledge base into the context once and reuses a precomputed KV cache (no retriever to fetch the wrong chunk), so it's *compile-time knowledge* (a frozen snapshot you rebuild wholesale on any edit) vs RAG's *runtime knowledge* (cheap incremental upsert); CAG only fits a bounded, slow-changing corpus that lives inside the window, and it inherits lost-in-the-middle as that corpus grows. Honest answer is hybrid (cache the hot static core, retrieve the volatile tail). Verified: arXiv 2412.15605 ("Don't Do RAG"), hhhuang/CAG repo (1.5k★, SQuAD/HotPotQA, its own stated limits), Lost-in-the-Middle 2307.03172, TurboRAG 2410.07590. (2) **GRPO vs PPO** — targets "grpo vs ppo" / "deepseek rl algorithm". Non-obvious thesis: GRPO won not as a better optimizer but because it **deletes a model** — PPO's critic is ~policy-sized, so removing it ~halves trainable-model memory, which is what moved RL post-training from a cluster to a single node; the cost relocated (needs G samples/prompt + a reward computable per complete output → why GRPO rose with verifiable-reward reasoning), and the group baseline brought its own biases (Dr. GRPO showed length-inflation), so "critic-free" isn't free. Verified: DeepSeekMath 2402.03300 (introduces GRPO), DeepSeek-R1 2501.12948, HF TRL GRPOTrainer docs, Dr. GRPO 2503.20783. Both rail correctly into existing clusters (cag-vs-rag→RAG & Retrieval, grpo-vs-ppo→Fine-Tuning & Training) — no taxonomy change needed; verified live sibling rails. **Part B (bug fix, #15/#29 internal-link SEO):** discovered the built/styled "In this piece" contents nav rendered on **0 of 243 posts** — `tocify` skipped any `<h2>` that already had an `id`, but markdown.js ids every heading, so `tocItems` was always empty and the gate never fired. Fixed tocify to honor + collect existing ids; relaxed the gate so section-rich pieces (≥5 `##`) get the nav regardless of read time. Result **0→68** posts now render a TOC with anchors that all resolve (jump-to anchors Google can surface as sitelinks on the demand money pages). Regression test pins it (TOC on >0 posts + every anchor resolves). Suite **856 green**; check:content slate clean (2 changed, 0 below). (1) AGENTS.md vs CLAUDE.md — targets "agents.md vs claude.md" / "what is AGENTS.md". Non-obvious thesis: these aren't faster computers, they're **differently-shaped memory systems** — token generation is HBM-bandwidth-bound, so all three keep weights in ~order-of-magnitude-faster on-chip SRAM; but SRAM is tiny (Groq ~500MB, Cerebras 44GB on one wafer, SambaNova 520MB + HBM/DDR tiers), so the real product is how many chips it takes to hold your model, and the speed edge only matters for **latency-bound interactive/agentic loops** — throughput-bound batch work still favors GPUs on vLLM per token. Figures stated as company-claim vs independent (Artificial Analysis ~1,758/693/476 tok/s on gpt-oss-120b; Cerebras claim 2,100/3,000; SambaNova ~250 on DeepSeek-R1 671B) — all canonical source URLs confirmed real via WebSearch (WebFetch is 403-blocked in this env). (1) PII redaction — targets "redact PII before sending to LLM" / "Presidio vs GLiNER". Non-obvious thesis: the detector choice is downstream of one architectural decision — *does the placeholder need to be reversible?* (an agent that must act on real data needs pseudonymization + a kept token-map, not deletion) — and "ask an LLM to strip the PII" is self-defeating because you must first send the raw PII to the very model you're shielding it from; recall is the metric since one miss is a leak. Verified @repo Presidio ~9.5k MIT / GLiNER ~3.3k Apache-2.0 + Presidio's built-in GLiNER recognizer; sources: Presidio docs (incl. encrypt/Decrypt reversibility), GLiNER NAACL-2024 paper 2311.08526, LangChain reversible anonymizer, hybrid multilingual PII study 2510.07551. (2) Prompt compression — targets "prompt compression LLMLingua" / "reduce LLM token cost". Non-obvious thesis: compression is itself a model call in front of your model call, so the saving is conditional (2026 "Prompt Compression in the Wild" 2604.02985 → speedup only inside a matched operating window) AND it *competes with prompt caching*, which wins on stable content (cache read ≈0.1× base input) — so the right pattern is cache the stable prefix, compress only the volatile non-cacheable RAG context. Verified microsoft/LLMLingua ~6.3k MIT, Selective Context ~423★ (stale since early-2024); sources: LLMLingua/LongLLMLingua/LLMLingua-2/Selective-Context papers, MS Research blog, Anthropic prompt-caching docs. | **Section-appropriate Article schema @type** (not blanket `NewsArticle`) | Run 22 Part B: every article emitted `NewsArticle` JSON-LD regardless of section. Google reserves `NewsArticle` for time-sensitive journalism (Top Stories eligibility, freshness-decay ranking signals); typing *evergreen* reference content as news works against durable ranking, and stamping satire as "NewsArticle" is structurally false. Fix (`render.js`): @type now follows the section — **The Wire → `NewsArticle`** (genuine AI news), **The Stack → `TechArticle`** (the type Google recommends for evergreen technical reference/comparisons — and the bulk of the demand corpus), **Dispatches & Fabrications → `Article`** (first-person essays / labeled satire, never news). All are `Article` subtypes so every other LD property (wordCount, keywords, timeRequired, author, publisher, breadcrumb) stays valid. | Cluster mis-home fix: stop Inference's decoding-`sampling` token from poaching reasoning test-time-compute pieces | The `/comparisons` clustering (council #15/#29) routes by an ordered first-match-wins regex, and a token meant for one concept silently poached another. This run's `self-consistency-vs-best-of-n-sampling` (a test-time-*compute* selection technique — the sibling of `sleep-time-compute-vs-test-time-compute` and `reasoning-effort-vs-thinking-budget`) was being bucketed into **Inference & Gateways**, because that cluster's bare `sampling` token (added for *decoding* sampling — temperature/top-p/top-k) matched the slug's `-sampling` suffix, and Inference precedes Agent Reasoning in the array so first-match-wins won before the reasoning cluster was ever checked. Root-caused that `sampling` was **redundant in Inference**: `temperature-vs-top-p-vs-top-k-llm-sampling` already homes via `temperature`/`top-p`/`top-k`, and `mcp-sampling-vs-elicitation` is caught earlier by Protocols' `mcp` — so the token helped nothing and only mis-homed reasoning pieces. Fix: removed `sampling` from **Inference & Gateways** and added bounded `self-consistency`/`best-of-n` to **Agent Reasoning & Planning** (corpus-scanned: both appear in only this one slug, so first-match-wins poaches nothing). Verified: self-consistency piece → Agent Reasoning (rails sleep-time/test-time + reasoning-effort); decoding-sampling money page stays in Inference; mcp-sampling unchanged. 1 regression test pins both the new home and the no-regression on the temperature piece. | Give every `compare:` table an accessible name via a topical `<caption>` (a11y + featured-snippet SEO) | WCAG / NYT / FT data tables — a data table needs a `<caption>` so a screen reader navigating by table announces *what* it compares, and a captioned table is stronger featured-snippet bait because the caption names the compared entities. Fix (lib/render.js): emit a `<caption>` as the table's first child, built from the header row's cells *after* the axis label ("Dimension"/"Platform"/"Approach") — i.e. exactly the compared options — as `"<opt> vs <opt> vs … — compared at a glance"` (HTML-escaped). Visually hidden (the visible `.cmp-head` "At a glance" `<p>` is untouched, so layout/CSS are unchanged) via inline `position:absolute;clip:rect(0 0 0 0)` styles — kept inline deliberately so the change is self-contained in render.js with no `style.css`/`style.min.css` sync risk. Purely additive: the `#25` `about`-entity extraction reads the `compare` *data* (not the HTML), so it's unaffected — verified `"about"` schema still emits. Header-only/empty ⇒ no caption (guarded on `opts.length`). Applies to all 252 demand pages at once. Extended the existing compare-table render test to pin the caption text (compared options, escaped), and that `<caption>` precedes `<thead>` (HTML requires caption first = the table's accessible name). Render-verified live on the new tools piece ("Tool search (defer_loading) vs Tool-RAG / RAG-MCP vs Code execution (progressive disclosure) — compared at a glance"). Dropped the dup in this run; then fixed the root cause: a `tokenMatch(x,y)` helper counts two tokens as the same when equal OR one is a prefix of the other with the shorter ≥ 4 chars, so abbreviation/full-form and morphological variants (eval⊂evaluation, optim⊂optimization, quant⊂quantization) collide while short tokens stay exact (`rag`⊄`ragas`, `mcp` exact). Corpus-validated before shipping: across all 397 posts the prefix rule flags the **same 6 pairs** as plain equality **plus** the eval/evaluation clone — **zero new false positives** (a false positive would wrongly block a legitimate new piece, so this was the safety bar). One regression test pins both directions (eval/evaluation clone → flagged; the ≥4 floor keeps adjacent rag subjects distinct). Suite 1288 green. | High | done | 2026-06-26 |
| Per-heading permalink anchors on every article (deep-link affordance) | MDN / Stripe docs / GitHub / Stratechery — a hover/focus-revealed `#` link on each section heading lets a reader copy a URL straight to that section, which raises shareability and feeds Google "jump to" sitelinks. The site already stamped stable `id`s on every `<h2>` (for the TOC, council #15/#29) and exposed them in the gated contents nav, but the heading itself carried **no visible anchor** — and the TOC only renders on long reads (`read_time >= 6 && >= 4 items`, or `>= 5 items`), so on the many 4-section pieces under that gate (e.g. this run's prompt-caching-pricing money page) there was *no* on-page way to deep-link a section at all. Fix: `tocify()` (lib/render.js) now appends a `headingAnchor(id)` (`<a class="heading-anchor" href="#id">#</a>`) inside every `<h2>` — both the already-id'd path (markdown-pipeline ids) and the fallback path — so the anchor targets the heading's own id on every article, TOC or not. Implemented as a precise, **reversible** enrichment mirroring `citeLinks` (exported `HEADING_ANCHOR_RE`), so the "body html embedded verbatim" render invariant still holds once stripped; the render test's normalization reverses both cite markers and heading anchors. CSS (style.css, the served file — render links `/style.css`, not the minified twin, so no min-sync risk): `.heading-anchor` is `opacity:0`, revealed on `h2:hover`/`:focus`, accent on hover, and held at `opacity:.5` under `@media (hover:none)` so it stays tappable on touch. 1 new regression test pins that every id'd body `<h2>` carries exactly one permalink whose target equals its own id (a dangling anchor would deep-link nowhere). | **Fresh safety Wire piece on context-compaction governance decay + homed into `/topics/agent-security` (#7/#14/#15/#17)** | 2026-07-01 run. **Part A (1 piece, 0 Dispatches — #7 cap):** the 537-post corpus is saturated on evergreen agent-dev queries, so the highest-value non-dup move was a *fresh, primary-sourced* Wire piece on a genuine gap. Filed `context-compaction-erases-agent-guardrails` targeting the query class **"does context compaction lose instructions" / "long-running agent forgets its rules" / "context compaction safety"**. Non-obvious thesis, corroborated across four independent 2026 sources: compaction is a *lossy, biased* summarizer — it keeps constraints that produce visible recent actions ("always log") and drops the ones defined by the *absence* of action ("never touch prod"), so a guardrail doing its job looks like silence and silence is the first thing a summarizer discards; safety therefore decays *fastest* when the agent has been behaving. Sourced numbers: Governance Decay (arXiv 2606.22528) — violation 0%→30% (59% worst model) across 1,323 episodes, 38% when the rule is dropped vs 0% when it survives, soft org policies decay 8.3× faster, a compaction-eviction *attack* defeats every model, Constraint Pinning restores 0% at <0.5% overhead; the omission-vs-commission asymmetry (arXiv 2604.20911, 73%→33% vs 100%); CWL structured eviction (arXiv 2606.11213); Anthropic's 84% token cut. Full demand-kit standard (4-bullet `summary`, 5 PAA `faq`→FAQPage, 6-row 4-col `compare`, 7 `figures`, 2 in-cluster body links, 4 sources; `art` void/ominous — a rules page folded smaller, the "never" lines fading to blank; PNG+WebP+AVIF committed). Auto-homes in **Guardrails & Safety** via the trailing `guardrails` token (15-piece rail). **Part B (product):** all 30 council moves remain ✅/🔵(owner-blocked) and every check is green (content-standard 404/0-below, freshness 0-stale, CWV 0-fail), so extended the proven head-term-hub pattern: wired the new piece into `SECURITY_HUB_SLUGS` (`lib/db.js`) in the attacks band right after `jailbreak-vs-prompt-injection` — both are "the agent stops obeying its own constraints" pieces — so `/topics/agent-security` funnels head-term equity to it, not just the auto-homed cluster rail. | **Fresh Wire piece on the LLM cascade pattern + made `cascade`/`frugalgpt` first-class in the cluster graph (#7/#14/#15/#17)** | 2026-07-02 run. **Part A (1 Wire piece, 0 Dispatches — #7 cap):** the corpus owned LLM *routers* (`routellm-vs-notdiamond-vs-martian`, the litellm/portkey gateways, `semantic-router-vs-llm-routing`) and *fallback chains* (`how-to-handle-llm-api-errors…`) but had **no page on the cascade pattern** — cheap model first, a verifier decides accept-or-escalate (FrugalGPT / AutoMix). Grep-confirmed gap: zero corpus bodies carried `frugalgpt`, and no `cascade` slug (only the voice-desk `…-cascaded-voice-agents`, a different sense). Filed `llm-cascade-vs-router` targeting **"llm cascade" / "model cascade vs router" / "frugalgpt"**. Non-obvious thesis: a router bets *before* it sees the answer, a cascade *after* — so a cascade's accuracy floor is the strong model (it can always escalate) but its cost floor is set by the **verifier**, not the models, and the verifier is the one component teams ship without benchmarking. A miscalibrated judge fails two opposite-and-both-expensive ways at once (false-accept → quality silently collapses to the weak model on exactly the hard queries; false-escalate → you pay cheap+verify+expensive to change nothing). Sourced: FrugalGPT (arXiv 2305.05176 — 98% cost cut / +4% at equal cost, DistilBERT scorer), AutoMix (2310.12963 — few-shot self-verify + POMDP), the 2026 decision-theoretic/calibration line (2605.06350 — 79.5% cut at 90% ceiling quality; 2605.18796), the adversarial-escalation attack surface (2605.17288), and a documented production incident where a provider formatting change broke a schema-verifier and escalated ~90% of traffic. Full demand-kit standard (4-bullet `summary`, 5 PAA `faq`→FAQPage, 8-row 4-col `compare` [router vs cascade vs single frontier], 4 `figures`, 3 in-cluster body links, 6 sources; `art` convergence/tense — a request stream filtered at a cheap gate, only the low-confidence few funneled to a costly tier; PNG+WebP+AVIF committed). **Part B (product #15/#29):** the new slug homes in **Inference & Gateways** today via its `router` token, but a FUTURE standalone `llm-model-cascade-*`/`frugalgpt-*` slug (no router/routing token) would have orphaned to the "More comparisons" catch-all. Added bounded `cascade`/`frugalgpt` to the Inference & Gateways cluster regex (`lib/db.js`), beside the routers they complement. Poaching-safe by construction: both tokens appear in no earlier cluster, and the bound stops `cascade` from matching `cascaded` (the voice slug stays in Voice Agents — asserted). Pinned with a `db.test.js` regression (cascade-vs-router + standalone cascade/frugalgpt → Inference & Gateways; cascaded-voice unmoved). Content-standard clean (closest existing piece only 13 shared tokens), freshness 0-stale, no dead links. **Full suite 1748→1751 green.** Env: fresh-clone `npm install` (in `app/`) needed `apt-get update && apt-get install -y libcairo2-dev libpango1.0-dev libjpeg-dev libgif-dev librsvg2-dev libpixman-1-dev` before canvas/better-sqlite3 compiled; run `ingest.js` before `gen-art.js`. `/api/analytics` + `dreaming.press` unreachable and `arxiv.org`/blog hosts 403 via the egress proxy, so demand + sourcing ran on corpus-gap analysis + live WebSearch (which works; exact figures cross-corroborated across independent snippets against confirmed-real arXiv IDs). | High | done | 2026-07-02 |
| **Fresh Wire piece on interleaved thinking (reasoning between tool calls) + caught & reverted a cannibalizing embedding-migration dup (#7/#14/#17)** | 2026-07-02 run. **Part A (1 Wire piece, 0 Dispatches — #7 cap):** filed `interleaved-thinking-agents-reason-between-tool-calls` targeting "interleaved thinking" / "should an agent reason between tool calls" — a genuine gap (grep-confirmed: no `interleaved*` slug; the phrase appears only in passing in `too-many-tools…` and `code-agents-vs-tool-calling-agents`). Non-obvious thesis: the payoff of thinking between tool calls is NOT a smarter first plan (a model plans fine up front) but **mid-trajectory error recovery** — noticing a tool returned an empty/error/contradicting result and re-planning before the mistake compounds down the chain; so it pays on long branching loops and is pure latency/token tax on fixed pipelines. Grounded in the current API surface (loaded the claude-api skill): on current Claude models **adaptive thinking auto-enables interleaved thinking** between tool calls, where earlier Claude 4 models needed the `interleaved-thinking-2025-05-14` beta header — so the real decision shifted from "enable it" to "is my task one where between-step reasoning earns its latency." Full demand-kit (4-bullet summary, 5 PAA faq→FAQPage, 6-row compare, 4 figures, 4 sources; art orbit/cold). **Editorial catch (quality > volume):** also drafted `embedding-model-migration-re-embedding`, pushed it, then on dedup review caught it **near-duplicates** the existing `how-to-migrate-embedding-models-in-production` (2026-06-25) — same two-incompatible-vector-spaces thesis, same "re-embed cost isn't the real cost" reframe, same Drift-Adapter climax and overlapping sources — and **reverted it** (post + 3 covers) before it could cannibalize the query.
